Introduction to NTA Level 4 Practicum Guide 
Overall Aim of the Practicum Guide 
The practicum guide has been developed to guide tutors to 
effectively train students in practicum sites to transfer 
theoretical knowledge into practice . It provides an 
opportunity for students to practice newly learned skills 
under the facilitation and supervision of tutors and clinical 
instructors. This practicum guide provides guidance on 
required areas for rotation, spe cific skills to be learnt and 
tasks to be completed. This guide promotes standardization 
of clinical medicine training in the practical and clinical 
settings. 
Layout and Organization of the Practicum Guide 
This practicum is organized into six major section s as 
follows: 
Section I:  Overview of the practicum which covers the 
overall aim of the practicum, definition of terms, structure, 
practicum areas, prerequisite, advance preparation, 
monitoring and evaluation. 
Section II: This section describes the clinical teaching team 
which includes tutors, clinical instructors, students and their 
roles and responsibilities. 

===== PAGE 8 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  7 
Section III:  This section explains clinical competencies 
according to NTA level 4, including the responsibilities of the 
graduate, summary of clinica l competencies, aims, 
clinical/practical learning objectives, clinical/practical 
opportunities and prerequisites.  
Section IV: This section contains a summary of all the 
practicum modules and their allotted time per week, 
including specific guidance for NT A Level 4 Semester I 
practicum modules. In each module consists of the overall 
aim, clinical learning objectives, competencies, practicum 
resources, prerequisites, clinical placement, specific 
activities/assignments, end of practicum assignment, 
monitoring and evaluation, and checklists specific to each 
practicum task. 
Section V: This section contains specific guidance for the 
Administrative Field Attachment that students during 
Semester I.  
Section VI:  This section contains summary of all the 
practicum mod ules and their allotted time per week, 
including specific guidance for NTA level 4 semester II 
practicum modules. Included in each module are the overall 
aim, clinical learning objectives, competencies, practicum

resources, prerequisites, clinical placemen t, specific 
activities/assignments, end of practicum assignment, 
monitoring and evaluation, and checklists specific to each 
practicum module.

appendix is a session that tutors can use to orient studen ts 
to the practicum guide before going to the clinical areas.

Users of this Practicum Guide 
This practicum guide is intended primarily for use by all tutors 
and clinical instructors who will assist students to acquire 
competences in the clinical setting. 
How Should the Practicum Guide be used? 
Health Training Institutions’ (HTIs) Responsibilities 
For an effective clinical teaching and learning, the HTIs 
should ensure: 
 Effective utilization of the National Guideline for Health 
Training Institutions and Tea ching Health Facilities 
(Clinical Placement of Health and Allied Sciences 
Students). 
 Availability of updated Memorandum of Understanding 
(MoU) with Teaching Health Facilities (THFs) to 
smoothen placement of students at practicum areas. 
 All Tutors and Clini cal instructors are oriented on 
practicum guides, assessment plans and practical 
procedure books. 
 There are adequate number and qualified clinical 
instructors. 
 Tutors participate in patient management and student 
teaching. 
 Proper utilization of Monitoring and evaluation tools for 
practical/clinical teaching and learning. 
 Early and timely distribution of Practical Procedure Books 
to students at the beginning of the semester. 
 Students have adequate clinical/practical exposure  as 
indicated in the curriculum

 Availability of adequate recommended Skills laboratory 
equipment, supplies and reagents 
 Effective utilization of Skills laboratory for teaching, 
learning and students’ assessment

Monitoring and Evaluation 
 The aim of monitoring and evaluation in this context is to 
monitor student progress in acquisition of skills. 
 Monitoring and evaluation will be done using checklists 
and end of assignment reports. 
 The checklist is a kind of task analysis describing what 
units of performance (step by step) are to be 
accomplished and  the performance requirements 
associated with each step. 
 The checklist determines whether students follow 
accepted steps in accomplishing required criteria 
(tasks/steps), how accurately they perform them, and in 
what sequence. 
 Checklists shall be used to s upport the student to grow 
and develop towards desired levels of clinical 
competence instead of using it as a formal assessment 
tool.  
  

===== PAGE 14 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  13 
Instructions for Completing Checklists 
 Each skill checklist contains a column on the far left side 
labelled ‘Standard’. The standards noted in this column 
relate to the overall procedure area. For each standard, 
there is an associated list of Criteria (steps/tasks) for 
students to complete in order to successfully meet the 
standard. 
 There are three columns in the checklist  labelled: 
‘Observed’, ‘Not Observed’ and ‘Not Applicable.’ For 
each criterion, instructors/tutors must tick ONE of the 
three boxes according to the guidance below: 
o Tick (√) ‘Observed’ if a student performs the criterion 
(step/task) correctly and in the proper sequence.  
o Tick (√) ‘Not Observed’ if a student does not perform 
the criterion (step/task) correctly; OR does not 
perform the task in the proper sequence; OR is  not 
observed performing a task that is able to be 
performed given the circumstances.  
 Note: Sometimes a criterion has sub -criteria. If 
there are multiple elements to a criterion and ANY 
part of the task is not observed, then ‘Not 
Observed’ should be ticked. 
 For example, the criterion ‘Assembles instruments 
for giving injection’ has the following sub-criteria: 
- Syringe and needles 
- Gloves 
- Swabs etc. 
 These criteria are not to be rated individually, but 
rather collectively. Therefore if one or more of the 
sub-criteria are not observed, then the whole 

===== PAGE 15 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  14 
criteria (Assembles instruments for giving 
injection) should be marked as ‘Not Observed.’ 
o For all ‘Not Observed’ criteria, the tutor/clinical 
instructor will note them in his/her supervision 
notebook and deliberate efforts will be made to create 
opportunities for the student to continue practicing the 
skill under support and supervision. 
o Tick (√) ‘Not Applicable’ if the criterion (step/task) 
either does not apply to a particular step/task,  OR 
could not be carried out by the student due to 
circumstances beyond his/her control (e.g. if 
necessary equipment is not available). 
o Write comments in the far right hand column labelled 
‘Comments’. Comments can be provided regardless 
of which box you tick. It is a space to include further 
information about the strengths or weaknesses of a 
student for each criterion; or to explain why a 
particular task w as not applicable during the 
observation. Remember to note comments in this 
column especially if you marked ‘Not Observed’ or 
‘Not Applicable’, as they will be useful for recalling 
details about the gap in skills or the reason why a 
given task/step was not  applicable. The comments 
should be very specific. General phrases such as 
poorly done should be avoided. 
 Note: The comments will be communicated to 
students immediately after observation in the 
clinical area, specifying learning problems and 
specific action plan to remedy the situation or help 
the student to learn the skill. 

===== PAGE 16 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  15 
 Tutors are advised to develop a clinical profile for each 
student so that whenever a tutor/clinical instructor meets 
with a student for a learning purpose, the tutor/clinical 
instructor will note the performance, strength and areas 
in which the student still needs assistance. This will also 
be helpful when tutors/clinical instructors are required to 
write clinical performance reports of students. 
 There are certain criteria which cut acr oss almost all 
tasks to be learned, including hand washing before and 
after each procedure, putting on the gloves and other 
‘Infection Prevention and Control’ (IPC) measures, 
confidentiality, providing privacy, and creating rapport. 
These may be seen as mo notonous or not directly 
relevant to a procedure, but are important for 
professionalism and preservation of patients’ rights. They 
need to be emphasized at every client/patient encounter. 
 After getting adequate practice of a certain skill, a student 
should ask the tutor/clinical instructor to assess his/her 
level of performance and sign the procedure book if the 
desired level of proficiency in that skill has been reached. 
 Prior to using checklists all stakeholders including 
tutors/clinical instructors and students should be oriented 
to the practicum guides thoroughly. 
 If a checklist does not meet the learning needs of your 
students in that particular location, make a note in the 
feedback forms at the Practicum Guide section, discuss 
it with the school administration. Such information will be 
useful during the review of the Practicum Guides. 
  

===== PAGE 17 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  16 
Practical Procedure Books 
 Students shall be provided with Practical Procedure 
Books at the beginning of each academic year. The 
Practicum Guide has been harmonised with the 
Practical Procedure Book as a guide to performing most 
of the procedures listed in the Practical Procedure Book. 
Students should be encouraged to practice skills at all 
the times when in clinical settings and use both the 
Practical Procedure Book and Practicum Guide for the 
acquisition of skills. 
 The tutor or a clinical instructor assigned for 
signing the student’s Practical Procedure Book 
should only do so after being satisfied that he/she 
has coached, supported and observed the student 
performing a task. 
 In order to help the student learn clinical skills more 
effectively tutors/clinical instructor must work with 
respective administrators to create an environment that 
is conducive for clinical learning, including creating 
opportunities for students to learn, ensuring the 
availability of working tools, providing immediate 
feedback and being available to them as a mentor. 

===== PAGE 18 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  17 
Section I: Overview of the Practicum 
Overall Aim of Clinical Practical Training 
The overall aim of the clinical practice and practical 
placement is to expose students to the entire range of 
activities in the health facili ty (out -patient, in -patient and 
laboratories) and field visits. While rotating through the 
different areas, students will acquire relevant skills to enable 
them to provid e or participate in the delivery of services at 
their respective levels. The practical training will be given 
over the entire instructional period of each semester. 
 
Purpose of the Practicum 
The practicum is a practical attachment intended to give 
students opportunities for supervised practical hands -on 
application of theoretical concepts studied in class during the 
current or past semester. The practicum may be a brief  
activity or could last longer. 
Clinical Rotation/Placement 
This is a form of practicum where students are attached to a 
clinical area for a specified period of time in order to learn the 
intended skills. At the site they have more interaction with 
patients and staff and have some responsibilities. During the 
clinical placement, responsibility for teaching relies on both 
clinical instructors and tutors. The practical time has to be 
utilized appropriately through organization of classroom and 
clinical hours for students to have enough time for effective 
acquisition of skills. 
  

===== PAGE 19 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  18 
Structure and Clinical Practicum Areas 
During NTA Level 4 S emester I, students will spend 5 1/2 
hours a week in the clinical setting. During NTA Level 4 
Semester II, students will spend 10 hours a week in the 
clinical setting.  
In order to have successfully completed the pr actical 
training, students should demonstrate competencies in 
Communication Skills and Customer Care, Human Anatomy, 
Epidemiology and Biostatistics,  Computer Applications, 
Microbiology, Parasitology and Entomology, Clinical 
Nutrition, Clinical Skills , Clinical Laboratory  and Patient 
Care.

o CMT 04212 Patient Care (40 practical hours)

Advance Preparation 
For all practical procedures, students should be introduced 
to the skills in class/skills laboratory through demonstrations, 
simulations and role plays. 
 
Clinical Teaching 
Tutors and clinical instructors have the responsibility to 
supervise, guide and teach students in the clinical setting. 
They also have responsibility of documenting student 
progress by making appropriate entries or endorsing 
procedures completed in the students’ Practical Procedure 
Books and relevant checklists. The roles and responsibilities 
of different staff in student learning are highlighted in section 
II of this practicum guide.

Section II: The Clinical Teaching Team and Their Roles 
Implementation of practical training requires the 
collaborative efforts of tutors, clinical instructors and 
students. Each of these groups has different roles in the 
teaching and learning process. The relationship between 
members of the clinical teaching team and students is 
important. Tutors, clinical instructors and students must 
communicate clearly with each oth er d uring the learning 
process. Students will gain the most from their experiences 
when feedback is given constructively in the spirit of 
promoting learning. Students are not expected to know how 
to do everything perfectly at once, but rather, they are in the 
process of learning to improve upon their current skills and 
learn new ones. The clinical instructor or tutor will work with 
each student or a group of students during the process of 
clinical learning and teaching. The tutor and clinical instructor 
will be  responsible to monitor, evaluate, and give 
constructive feedback on students’ learning. The students 
will have the opportunity to seek guidance from and provide 
feedback to the tutors and clinical instructors.

Roles and Responsibilities of a Tutor in Clinical 
Teaching 
The main role of a tutor is to plan and coordinate the 
placement of students and teach students in the clinical 
settings. He/she will have the following roles in guiding 
students:  
 Ensure that students have completed prerequisite 
classroom sessions prior to starting the relevant 
practicum  
 Develop a curriculum cum rotation plan  
 Decide on the times during which students should go to 
the practicum 
 Identify practicum sites 
 Identify clinical instructors for specific skills 
 Plan for clinical teaching which indicates who, what, 
when and how to guide students in the clinical area 
 Set clinical objectives for each placement  
o These objectives can be set at the begin ning of the 
clinical placement and reviewed regularly 
 Communicate curriculum cum rotation plan to students 
and provide Practicum Guide and Practical Procedure 
Books to each student 
 Meet with relevant clinical instructors and non -clinical 
staff to orient them on: 
o The clinical objectives, competencies and skills which 
students are expected to achieve while in the clinical 
setting 
o The specific assignments and student assessment 
tools e.g. checklists for each placement 
o Grading system as based on the curriculum 

===== PAGE 26 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  25 
 Discuss with hospital administration to agree on how the 
groups should be divided  
o Students should go in small groups to the outpatient 
department, inpatient wards and other areas as 
necessary 
 Create a schedule for the practicum and share this with 
students, clinical instructors and administration 
o Should be based on discussions, group sizes, timing, 
and availability of relevant staff 
 Visit the students in the clinical area for clinical teaching 
and guidance  
 Document all activities done by the students and monitor 
progress 
 Plan for clinical practice evaluation after making sure that 
the students have acquired the necessary skills 
 Give feedback correctly, fairly and constructively 
o Giving constructive feedback means pointing out the 
things that the student does correctly as well as things 
for the student to improve on 
 Organise training resources to be used in the practicum 
 Liaise with clinical instructors on a daily basis to ensure 
that students are acquiring competencies when rotating 
in the different departments 
 Liaise with clinical instructors to continuously assess 
student abilities. 
 Introduce students to clinical instructors and other non 
teaching clinical staff  
 
Roles and Responsibilities of the Clinical Instructor 

===== PAGE 27 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  26 
At every clinical placement site, clinical instructors should be 
identified to directly guide students achieve competencies in 
specific areas. The following are their roles and 
responsibilities: 
 Receive and orient students to the clinical area  
 Introduce students to patients and other staff 
 Assign students to specific tasks 
 Involve students during management and care of 
patients/clients 
 Work with the students to set daily clinical learning 
objectives and plan 
 Teach and guide students to learn the intended skills 
 Demonstrate  and provide regular guidance to students 
 Give constructive feedback to students and complete 
student checklist based on participation in activities in 
different areas: 
o Giving constructive feedback means pointing out the 
things that the student does correctly as well as things 
for the student to improve on 
 Act as a good role model for professional conduct 
 Meet with students and tutors to discuss student progress 
and clinical challenges 
 Document student achievements in their Practical 
Procedure Books and assessment tools, e.g. checklists 
 Participate in student clinical monitoring and evaluation 
 Write reports and submit them to the institution on 
students performances and progress  
Roles and Responsibilities of Non-teaching Clinical 
Staff (other staff in health facilities) 

===== PAGE 28 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  27 
Non-teaching clinical staff in the different areas should 
facilitate student learning by assisting students to achieve 
their objectives. The non - teaching clinical staff will be 
expected to: 
 Facilitate integration of the students into clinical sites 
 Provide students with relevant tools and materials 
 Show students the best ways of behaving in clinical sites 
 
Roles and Responsibilities of the Student in Clinical 
Setting 
Students are expected to show diligence in clinical areas and 
behave in a way that is consistent w ith ethical practice. 
Students will have the following roles: 
 Learn the clinical setting leadership, policies, routine and 
procedures 
 Learn clinical skills according to clinical learning 
objectives 
 Present patient summaries and case studies and other 
assignments to clinical instructors and tutors as relevant 
 Perform other duties as assigned by clinical 
staff/instructors at the relevant site 
 Complete assignments and submit them to the relevant 
clinical instructor or tutor as appropriate 
 Maintain medical records correctly 
 Support fellow students in the clinical areas for effective 
learning 
 Share clinical learning experiences in classroom and 
clinical areas in case presentations and clinical 
conferences 
 Receive feedback from tutors and clinical instructors on 
progress and discuss/ask questions 
 Provide feedback to tutors and clinical instructors on 
clinical teaching and learning process 
NOTE: Other specific roles will depend on the specific area 
in which the student will be placed. 

===== PAGE 29 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  28 
 
Patient/Client as a Resource 
Patient/Client is a very important resource and plays a key 
role in the clinical learning of students. He/she is a constant 
resource for students as source of information and students’ 
feedback. However, the patient/client has rights which tutor, 
clinical staff/instructor and student have to observe as they 
interact with the patient/client. These rights include: 
 Being treated with compassion, love and respect. 
 Getting correct information about his/her health 
conditions, prescribed treatment and procedures. 
 Complaining and appealing in accordance with 
established procedures. 
 The right to privacy and confidentiality. 
 Refusing services if they do not meet the required needs 
and standards. 
 Accessing health services, facilities and information 
according to their needs. 
 The right for informed consent, self -expression and 
choice of care. 
 Knowing health facility rules and regulations that apply to 
his/her conduct as a patient.

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  29 
Section III. Clinical Competencies According to NTA 
Level 4 
Introduction 
NTA Level 4 graduates (Basic Technicians) are expected to 
work at a healthy facility to assist health workers in provision 
of preventive, curative, diagnostic and administrative 
services. 
 
Table 1: Summary of Clinical Competencies According 
to NTA Level 4 
 Principal Outcomes 
By the end of the NTA Level 4 rotation students will be able to: 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Biostatistics, Computer and 
Health Management Information Systems in provision of 
health care services 
 Apply knowledge and skills of medical ethics and 
professionalism, communication and customer care in 
provision of quality health care services 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Basic Medical Sciences and 
Epidemiology in diagnosing and preventing diseases 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in caring 
clients at Primary Health Care facility 
 Apply knowledge and skills of abnormal tissues changes 
and diagnostic methods in detecting diseases

NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  31 
Aim 
The overall aim of the NTA Level 4 practicum is to enable a 
student to acquire the necessary clinical medicine and 
communication competencies to confidently solve day -to-
day problems in health c are settings. Specifically, students 
will be prepared to continue on to NTA Levels 5. To this end, 
the following outcomes have been established for students: 
 Identify structural organization of human body  
 Use communication and customer care skills in deli very 
health services 
 Identify microorganisms, parasites and vectors of medical 
importance  
 Manage and utilize health data  
 Use basic computer applications 
 Demonstrate techniques for clinical skills practice 
 Assist in provision of patient care  
 Practice Infection Prevention and Control  (IPC) 
 Utilize knowledge of nutrition to manage diseases

to be able to:

data.

communications and patient information 
 Apply principles of health management information 
system in managing health data

clients/patients

providing emergency care services and first aid 
 Apply knowledge and skills of rapid diagnostic test in 
detecting diseases

health care services

health care services

Clinical/Practical Opportunities  
By end of NTA level 4 training students are expected to be 
able to:

body

vectors of medical importance 
 Learn and practice principles of communication skills and 
customer care 
Prerequisites 
Before starting the practicum, students should have had 
theoretical preparation. However for this level, students can 
go to clinical areas at the same time as they are receiving 
didactic training. These exposures are brief and related to 
the particular area covered in class.

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  36 
 Practical learning outcomes 
 Practical assignment sheets 
 
Prerequisite modules 
 None 
Clinical Placement 
During placement in Medical ward,  RCH Clinic, Paediatric 
ward, OPD and other relevant departments, students will get 
the opportunity to gain communication and customer care 
skills through activities, assignments and demonstrations. In 
each area students will have an opportunity to observe and 
acquire different skills and to see how those skills are applied 
in different settings. 
 
Activity1: Classroom 
Divide student in small manageable groups (4-6) and assign an 
activity of preparing one official letter indicating writers address, 
recipient organization/ person address, date, reference number, 
title, main message, signature of the writer.   
Submit to the tutor and present in plenary. 
 
Activity 2: Classroom 
Divide student in small manageable groups(4-6) and assign an 
activity of  preparing one curriculum vitae indicating personal 
particular, education background, field/working experience (if 
applicable), hobbies and two referees and submit to the instructor 
Submit to the tutor and present in plenary. 

===== PAGE 38 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine  37 
Activity 3: Medical Ward, Paediatric ward, OPD and Relevant 
Areas 
In the above mentioned areas, Divide student in small manageable 
groups(4-6) and assign activities of communication as follows: 
Assignment 1 
Instruct s tudent to apply skills of effective communication in 
communicating with at least one client, either child or adolescent. 
Write and submit to the instructor a report showing the problem of the 
client, message shared, barriers during communication, ways of 
communication used and client’s feedba ck and duration of the 
session. 
Assignment 2 
Instruct student to apply skills of effective communication in 
communicating with at least one client with either speech, hearing, 
visual or mental disabilities. Write and submit to the instructor a report 
showing the problem of the client, message shared, ways of 
communication used and barriers during communication, client’s 
feedback and duration of the session.   
 
Assignment 3 
Instruct student to apply skills of effective co mmunication in 
communicating with at least one client, either drug abusers or 
alcoholics. Write and submit to the instructor a report showing the 
problem of the client, message shared, ways of communication used 
and barriers during communication, client’s feedback and duration of 
the session. 
Assignment 4 
Instruct student to apply skills of customer care with at least one client 
receiving health services. Write and submit to the instructor a report 
showing the customer satisfaction with services delivered, language 
of health care provider, and time of services and environment of the 
facility.

NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 52 
Practicum Module: CMT04104 Epidemiology and 
Biostatistics 
 
 
 
Total Practical Time:20 hours in 20 weeks of 
a semester  
 
Aim: The aim of this practicum is for students to acquire and 
apply skills in managing health information. 
 
Practical Learning Outcomes: 
By the end of practicum the students will be able to: 
 Utilize biostatistical tools used in data collection 
 Utilize basic statistical method in compiling health data 
 Use biostatistical data from Health Management 
Information system for data analysis 
 Use different methods of presentation to present 
analysed data. 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Develop tools for data collection 
 Use biostatistical tools for data collection 
 Compile health data using appropriate statistical methods 
 Use HMIS tools to collect data  
 Use different data analysis tools 
 Present data using (tables, charts, maps/pictorials, 
narrative)  
 Summarize HMIS data using tally sheet forms  
 Use different biostatistical methods to analyze data 
 Generate monthly and hospital summary reports

6.1.22 Column (22): Mother/child status during discharge.  
 Writes mother’s status at discharge from the maternity

maternity ward.

parts.

year. Therefore 10 is the value enters for every child 
registered during the year 2010. This is entered in the 
first part of the column.  
 The second part of the identification number enters 
the unique number given to each child at registration. 
Each child is numbered sequentially, with the first 
child being assigned number 001.

line observed in MRDT Kit 
 Pf: Enter tick (√) in appropriate column of Pf if Pf line observed in 
MRDT Kit

 Pan: Enter tick (√) in appropriate column of Pan if Pan line

===== PAGE 130 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 129 
Practicum Module: CMT04105 Computer Applications 
 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 40 hours in 20 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of this practicum is for students to apply basic 
computer skills in managing health facility communication 
and patient information. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes: 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able to: 
 Apply basic concept of a computer system 
 Apply Word processing program in keeping records 
 Apply spread sheet program in keeping records 
 Use presentation application in information dissemination  
 Apply internet & email skills in accessing medical records 
 Apply planned preventive maintenance of computer 
 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Perform computer connections and start up (start a 
computer, launch, open programs, perform data entry, 
text via key board & mouse and turn off the computer) 
 Manage folders and files  
 Transfer file from computer to removable disks 
 Launch/ start basic computer programs and software 
 Create and format documents  
 Manipulate an office document  


===== PAGE 131 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 130 
 Print documents 
 Use internet and w eb in searching and downloading 
materials 
 Create an Email account and manage messages 
 Send files through E-mail 
 Employ PPM procedures to protect computer 
 
Practical learning resources 
 Computers and accessories  
 Internet connection 
 LCD projector 
 Assignment sheets 
 Checklists  
 
Prerequisites 
 None  
 
Advance Preparation: 
The tutor should liaise with the relevant authorities to ensure 
that the necessary practicum resources (listed above) are in 
place and working. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

===== PAGE 132 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 131 
Activity 1: Institution Computer laboratory  
In the above m entioned area, A ssigned students to the 
activities of data maintenance, report generation, and email.  
Divided students into small manageable groups to work with 
the ICT Officer/Tutor  of the training institution to perform the 
following: 
 Enter data in the computer and generate a data report 
using word processors 
 Enter data in a spreadsheet program and generate a data 
report  
 Represent data in graphs using Excel 
 Enter data in the computer and prepare presentations 
using Microsoft power point  
 Create an email account, sign in, send email to supervisor 
with attachment 
 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Instructors and tutors will make daily observations of 
students, provide support and document progress. The 
instructors and tutors will give timely fee dback for student’s 
improvement.

177 
Practicum Module: CMT 04107 Microbiology, 
Parasitology and Entomology 
 
 
Total Practical Time: During theory 
sessions in 20 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The goal of practicum in Micr obiology, Parasitology 
and Entomology is for students to acquire and apply basic 
laboratory skills of identifying microorganisms, parasites 
and vectors of medical importance. 
 
Practical Learning Outcomes: 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to b e able 
to: 
 Apply knowledge of microorganisms of medical 
importance in detecting causes of disease  
 Apply knowledge of parasites and vectors of medical 
importance in detecting causes of disease 
 
Competencies/Skills: 
 Identify microorganisms, parasites and ve ctors of 
medical importance 
 Report the findings after identification 
 Identify normal flora found in different body parts 
 Identify morphological appearance of Gram positive and 
Gram negative of medical importance 
 Identify specimens which can be collected to  identify 
gram positive and gram negative of medical importance. 


===== PAGE 179 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
178 
 Identify specimens used to identify mycobacteria and 
spirochetes of medical importance 
 Collect specimens which can be used to detect RNA 
and DNA viruses 
 Identify fungi of medical importance 
 Identify parasite of medical importance 
 Identify vectors of medical importance 
 Report the find after identification 
 
Practicum Resources: 
 Practical learning outcomes 
 Practical rotation plan 
 Checklists  
 All necessary working tools/supplies  
 
 
Prerequisite modules 
 None 
 
 
Practical Attachment 
During the practical attachment in clinical laboratory, 
students will get opportunities to learn skills in microbiology, 
parasitology and entomology. Students will have an 
opportunity to observe, practice and acquire diffe rent skills 
under supervision in the above mentioned areas.  
 
 
 

===== PAGE 180 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
179 
Activity 1: Microbiology Unit/Section 
Divided students in small manageable groups and instruct to perform the 
following tasks: 
 Examine prepared slides for microscopy identification for the 
following bacteria: 
Streptococcus, Staphylococcus, Clostridium tetani, Bacillus 
anthracis, Corynebacteria, Neisseria, Salmonella, Shigella, 
Escherichia coli, Haemophilus, Vibrio cholera, Mycobacteria. 
 Examine prepared slides for microscopy identification for the 
following fungi: 
Candida albicans, Cryptococcus neoformans. 
 Report the findings 
 
 
Activity 2: Parasitology Unit/Section  
Divided Students in small manageable groups and instruct to perform the 
following tasks: 
 Examine prepared blood slide smears for blood parasites: 
Plasmodium, Trypanosoma, and Microfilaria of  Wuchereria 
bancrofti, Onchocerca volvulus for microscopy identification 
 Examine prepared urine slide smears for microscopic 
identification of: 
Schistosoma haematobium and Trichomonas vaginalis  
 Examine prepared stool slide smears for microscopic identification 
of: 
Schistosoma mansoni, Schistosoma japonicum, Taenia, 
Echinococcus, Hookworms, Trichuris trichiura, Ascaris 
lumbricoides, Strongyloides stercoralis, Enterobius 
vermicularis, Giardia lamblia, Entamoeba histolytica. 
 Identify macroscopic features of parasites 
 Report the findings 

===== PAGE 181 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
180 
 
 
 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Tutors will make necessary preparations, make daily 
observations of students, provide support and document 
progress using check list provided. Tutors will give timely 
feedback for student’s improvement. 
The report shall be assessed and graded; then immediate 
feedback shall be given to the student. 
 
 
 
 
Activity 3: Entomology Unit/Section  
Divided Students in small manageable groups and instruct to perform the 
following tasks: 
 Identify morphological features; Mosquitoes, Tsetse flies, 
Blackflies, Lice, Ticks, Fleas, Bedbugs, Mites and Snails 
 Report the findings

183 
Practicum Module: CMT04208 Clinical Nutrition 
 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 20 hours in 20 weeks 
of a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of practicum is for students to acquire 
knowledge and skills of nutrition in diagnosing and 
preventing diseases 
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes: 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Apply knowledge of nutrition in diagnosing and 
preventing diseases 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Obtain client’s dietary history 
 Perform anthropometric measurements  
 Infer nutritional assessment results using standardized 
references 
 Provide nutritional advice to clients as appropriate 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Practicum checklist 
 Practical placement plan 
 Practical learning outcome 
 Clinical assessment tools 
 
  


===== PAGE 185 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
184 
Prerequisites modules: 
 None 
 
Clinical Placement 
During placement in medical, paediatrics, OPD and other 
relevant areas students will get the opportunity to acquire 
knowledge and skills of nutrition in diagnosing and 
preventing diseases. 
 
Activity 1: Medical, RCH, Paediatrics, OPD and Relevant Areas 
In the above mentioned areas, Assigned students to the activities of 
obtaining client’s dietary history, performing anthropometric 
measurements and providing nutritional advice to clients as appropriate.    
 
Assignment 1 
Instruct students to obtain dietary history of  the following groups:  
 Children aged 0-6 months, 6-24 months and children aged above 2 
years. 
 Pregnant, lactating mothers and people with illnesses. 
Write a report and present during plenary 
Note: Time for the session should not exceed 15minutes. 
 
Assignment 2 
Instruct students to perform anthropometric measurements to identify 
weight for height/length, weight for age, height/length for age, Mid 
Upper Arm Circumference (MUAC) and Body mass index (BMI). Then 
infer the results obtained with standardized references. 
 Write and submit a report and present in plenary 
 
Assignment 3 
Instruct students to conduct two nutrition health education/counselling 
sessions, one to a child caretaker/parent on exclusive breast feeding/ 

===== PAGE 186 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
185 
complimentary feeding and one to adult client on eating varieties of food 
and balanced diet and how to plan meals to include all food groups. 
Write and submit a report showing problem/ need of the client, key 
messages and agreed goals and way forward.  
Note: Time for the session should not exceed 15minutes 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklists provided below. Clinical instructors and tutors will 
give timely feedback for student’s improvement. Reports 
shall be assessed and graded; then immediate feedback 
shall be given to the student.

6 – 23 months old

 Hooks the spring scale to a tripod or a stick held

board.

 Gently holds the child’s head so eyes point

 If the client is a very young child, sits down so

202 
Practicum Module: CMT 04209 Clinical Skills 
 
 
 
Total Practical Time:80 hours in 
20weeks of a 
semester 
 
Aim: The aim of the clinical practice is for students to 
utilize competences in clinical skills to assess patients.  
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes  
By the end of this practicum, students are expected to 
be able to: 
 Apply communication skills to build a therapeutic 
relationship with a client 
 Apply communication skills in taking history of a 
patient/client 
 Conduct Patient Physical Examination

Clinical Placement 
During placement in IPD and OPD departments 
students will get the opportunity to observe and practice 
clinical skills. Through assignments and 
demonstrations, students will acquire skills and see how 
those skills are applied in diverse settings.  
 
Activity 1: Outpatient and Inpatient Departments  
Instruct student to  take patient history, p erform physical 
examination and interpret findings.   
Assignment 1 
 For each patient/client the student shall make relevant 
documentation, prepare a presentation and share 
with the clinical team and other students.

204 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical ins tructors and tutors will make daily 
observations of students, provide support and 
document progress using checklists provided.  The 
clinical instructors and tutors will give timely feedback 
for student’s improvement. The reports shall be 
assessed and grade d; then immediate feedback shall 
be given to the student.

changes in colour, changes in hair ornails.

stiffness in the neck.

27.1 Performin
g  
general 
examinati
on 
27.1.1 Makes quick assessment (e.g. alertness, lethargy, 
weak and unconsciousness ) 
    
27.1.2 For paediatric patients/clients Measures 
anthropometric parameter (refer to the respective 
Checklist) 
    
27.1.3 Checks Vital Signs (refer to the respective Checklist)     
27.1.4 Removes and wipes the thermometer with cotton 
wool to enable reading while avoiding touching the 
mercury part 
    
27.1.5 Reads thermometer at  eye level before shaking     
27.1.6 Clean the thermometer with antiseptic      
27.1.7 Shakes the thermometer until the mercury line is 
below 98.6° F (37° C). 
    

===== PAGE 227 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 226 
Skills 
Observed 
Not 
Observed Not 
Applicable 
Comments 
Standard Criteria 
27.1.8 Records and Interprets the findings      
27.1.9 Checking pulse rate 
 Identifies anatomical areas for taking pulse (e.g., 
radial, brachial, carotid) 
    
27.1.10 Places the three mid fingers over the artery( in case 
of the radial pulse) using slight but steady pressure 
    
27.1.11 Feels for pulse and establishes volume and rhythm     
27.1.12 Counts the pulse rate for one full minute using a 
watch and synchronises with other peripheral 
pulses 
    
27.1.13 Record and interpret findings in patient/client chart     
27.1.14 Checking for respirations 
 While fingers are still the site of checking pulse, 
observes respiration by looking at chest movements

27.1.15 Counts respiration rate for one full minute     
27.1.16 Records findings in patient/client chart and 
interprets the findings 
    
27.1.17 Checking blood pressure 
 Places the patient/client in an appropriate position to 
measure blood pressure by positioning the arm 
properly 
    
27.1.18 Identifies anatomical area for applying a cuff on the 
extended upper arm 
    
27.1.19 Applies a cuff at the middle of the upper arm     
27.1.20 Feels for brachial artery pulsation     
27.1.21 Inflates cuff until the arterial pulsations are no 
longer felt

Checklist 28: Performing local examination 
29.1 Performing 
local 
examination 
29.2. 1 Explains to the patient about the examination     
29.2. 2 Maintains privacy      
29.2. 3 Asks for consent      
29.2. 4 Washes hands with soap and water      
29.2. 5 Puts on gloves (when necessary)     
29.2. 6 Inspects the lesion on affected site and notes 
number, abnormal colour, size, shape, 
surrounding skin,  bleeding and/or discharge  
    
29.2. 7 Palpates the affected part and notes signs of 
inflammation, and abnormal movement  
    
29.2. 8 Percusses the affected part (if indicated)     
29.2. 9 Auscultates the affected part (if indicated)     
29.2. 10 Watches patient’s reaction during 
examination  
    
29.2. 11 Thanks the patient     
29.2. 12 Washes hands with soap and water

31.3.18 Locating the liver:      

===== PAGE 257 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 256 
 Places the right hand/use both hands on the right 
iliac fossa of the patient/client. 
 Exerts gentle pressure and asks the patient/client 
to take a deep breath (The liver will move 
downward due to the downward movement of the 
diaphragm). 
 Palpate using the caudal aspect of the fingers 
moving upward 2-3 cm at a time towards the 
lower costal margin, each time instructs the 
patient to take a deep breath. 
 Feels if the edges of the liver is moving 
downward and under the examining hand 
 Liver Span Assessment 
 If palpable, percusses from the second 
intercoastal space along the mid-clavicular line 
until the point of dullness. Note the site of 
dullness; continue purcassing till the dull note 
disappears. Note the point of disappearance of 
the dull sound. Measure the length between the 
two points

32.6.11. Weber's test: Lateralization

 When stop hearing it, moves to the patient’s ear

examine palate for uvular displacement.  
 Instructs the Patient to say ‘Ahhh’: symmetrical soft 
palate movement 
 Uses wooden spatula to elicit Gag reflex (Stimulate 
back of throat each side) 
CN XI: Accessory

 From behind, examines for trapezius atrophy, 
asymmetry 
 Instructs the Patient to shrug shoulders (trapezius)

278 
Practicum Module: CMT 04211 Clinical Laboratory 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 40 hours in 20 
weeks of a semester 
 
Aim: The goal of practicum in Clinical Laboratory is for 
students to acquire and apply basic laboratory skills at 
the health facility. 
 
Practical Learning Outcome: 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be 
able to: 
 Utilize procedures of Hand hygiene in preventing 
and controlling infections 
 Use techniques of personal protection in preventing 
and controlling infections at health care setting 
 Apply principles of occupational exposure in 
preventing and controlling infections among health 
care workers  
 Utilize procedures of health care waste management 
in preventing and controlling infections 
 Employ procedures of p rocessing equipment and 
materials in preventing and controlling infections 
 Use medical laboratory techniques in collecting 
specimen 
 Perform basic laboratory procedures for disease 
diagnosis 


===== PAGE 280 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
279 
Competencies/Skills: 
 Perform hand hygiene techniques according to 
standard operating procedures 
 Don and doff personal protective equipment 
according to operating procedures 
 Perform exposure site management  
 Provide care to HIV and Hepatitis B and C exposed 
health care worker 
 Manage health care wastes 
 Prepare solution for  decontamination and 
disinfection 
 Use of autoclave for sterilization 
 Perform decontamination and sterilization 
 Demonstrate skills when communicating to patients 
about the  laboratory procedure  to be undertaken 
 Collect specimens from patients  
 Store specimens for different rapid tests 
 Label and dispatch specimen to laboratory 
 Perform different laboratory tests  
 Interpret laboratory test results and inform the 
patient 
 
 
Practicum Resources: 
 Practical learning outcomes 
 Practical rotation plan 
 Practical assignment sheets 

===== PAGE 281 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
280 
 Checklists  
 All necessary working tools/supplies  
Prerequisite modules 
 Communication Skills and Customer care 
 Human Anatomy 
 Microbiology, Parasitology and Entomology 
 
Practical Attachment 
During the practical attachment in hospital laboratory, 
students will get opportunities to learn skills in reception, 
haematology, parasitology and microbiology. Students 
will have an opportunity to observe, practice, and 
acquire different skills under supervision in the above 
mentioned areas.  
Also students will get the opportunity to utilize principles 
of infection prevention and control to prevent infections 
in the IPD, OPD and other relevant hospital areas. 
Activity 1: IPD, OPD and Other Relevant Areas 
In the above mentioned areas, Divided students small manageable 
groups (4-6) and assign to the activity of procedures of infection, 
prevention control.   
 
Assignment  
Instruct each student to demonstrate skills while performing 
procedures related to infection prevention and control such as: hand 
washing, use of PPE, antiseptics and disinfectants.  
 
  

===== PAGE 282 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
281 
Activity 2: Laboratory Reception Section 
Assign each student to visit a laboratory reception unit to observe and 
practice the following: 
 Appropriate selection of containers for specimen collection 
 Collection of specimen correctly 
 Instruct patients on how to collect own specimen of urine 
 Proper handling of the specimen 
 Examine blood for glucose 
 Examine urine for analytes 
 Proper handling of plastic and glass ware 
 Proper handling of chemicals and reagents 
 Disposal of contaminated materials and sharps 
 Proper use of Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) 
 
Activity 3: Haematology Unit/Section  
Assign each student to visit a laboratory hematology unit to observe 
and practice the following: 
 Estimate haemoglobin level  
 Interpret the results (Low, Normal and high Haemoglobin 
concentration)  
 Handle blood specimens  
 Proper handling of chemicals and reagents  
 Disposal of contaminated materials and sharps 
 Proper use of Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) 
Assignment  
Instruct each student to perform at least three (3) haemoglobin level 
estimation, report and interpret the results 
 
Activity 4: Microbiology Unit/Section 
Assign each student to visit a laboratory microbiology unit to 
observe and practice the following: 

===== PAGE 283 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
282 
 Perform HIV, HBV, HCV Syphilis, Helicobacter pylori rapid 
tests 
 Proper handling of chemicals and reagents 
 Disposal of contaminated materials and sharps 
 Proper use of Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) 
Assignment  
Instruct each student to perform at least five (5) each of HIV, 
HBV, HCV Syphilis, Helicobacter pylori rapid tests, report and 
interpret the results 
 
Activity 5: Parasitology Unit/Section  
Assign each student to visit a laboratory parasitology unit to 
observe and practice the following: 
 Perform malaria rapid test 
 Proper handling of chemicals and reagents 
 Proper disposal of contaminated materials and sharps 
 Proper use of PPE  
Assignment  
Instruct each student to perform at least five (5) malaria rapid test, 
report and interpret the results  
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical Instructors and tutors will make necessary 
preparations, make daily observations of students, 
provide support and document progress using checklist 
provided. Clinical instructors and tutors will give timely 
feedback for student’s improvement. 
The report shall be assessed and graded; then 
immediate feedback shall be given to the student.

Skills

s Standard Criteria 
35 Use of 
Autoclave 
machine 
35.1.1 Place the Autoclave and sterilizing material on the 
table or on the shelf 
    
35.1.2 Remove the stopcock whistle/valve from the vent 
tube fitted at the lid 
    
35.1.3 Twist the lid of the autoclave Anticlockwise and lift 
the lid 
    
35.1.4 Fill water (appx. 1 liter) in the base chamber     
35.1.5 Place the loaded Basket/Dressing Drum in the 
autoclave 
    
35.1.6 Check that the rubber seal is in place and is in good 
condition 
    
35.1.7 Put on the lid, press it down and turn it clockwise 
until it does not turn anymore 
    
35.1.8 The sterilizer in the closed position will have both 
handles parallel and one above the other on either 
side of the stericlave. 
    

===== PAGE 292 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 291 
Skills 
Observed 
Not Observed 
Not Applicable 
Comment
s Standard Criteria 
35.1.9 Put and press down the Stopcock whistle/valve on 
the vent tube on the lid and turn on the power. 
    
35.1.10 After few minutes steam will start generating and at 
15±2 lb psi pressure the steam will come out 
strongly from the stopcock. 
    
35.1.11 Let the process go on and after 15minutes turn off 
the power and lift or pull out the Stopcock 
whistle/valve of the vent tube with forceps or piece 
of cloth. 
    
35.1.12 The whole steam will escape but it will be hot.     
35.1.13 Make sure that there is no steam left in the 
sterilizer and pressure gauge is also at Zero lb psi. 
    
35.1.14 Now open the autoclave lid by twisting it 
anticlockwise and lift the lid off the base chamber. 
    
35.1.15 Remove the rack/dressing drum from the autoclave 
and Preserve them in a safe place.

===== PAGE 337 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 336 
 
Practicum Module: CMT 04212: Patient Care 
 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 40 hours in 20weeks of a 
semester 
 
Aim: The aim of the practicum is for students to apply knowledge and 
skills of patient care in managing clients.  
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes 
 Utilize skills of patient care in positioning patients 
 Utilize skills of patient care in administering medications in 
different routes 
 Utilize skills  of patient care in preparing trays for clinical 
procedures 
 Apply principles of emergency care in managing patients with 
difficulty in breathing, convulsion, bleeding, unconscious patients, 
heart attack and cardiac arrest. 
 Apply principles of first aid in managing clients 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Place the patient in respective medical position  
 Perform drug administration depending on the route 
 Administer Oral medication 
 Administer intra muscular injection 
 Administer intravenous injection 
 Administer subcutaneous injection 
 Administer topical medication 


===== PAGE 338 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 337 
 Prepare a tray for common medical and surgical procedures 
 Administer oxygen therapy for a patient with difficulty breathing  
 Administer per rectal diazepam to a convulsing patient  
 Administer Intravenous fluid to a patient in shock due to bleeding   
 Position unconscious patient in a recovery position  
 Perform basic life support (CPR) 
 Prepare first aid kit 
 Provide first aid 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Practicum checklists  
 Clinical learning outcomes  
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical assignment sheets 
 All other necessary equipments and supplies  
 
Prerequisite modules 
 All modules of NTA Level 4 Semester I 
 
Clinical Placement 
During placement in skills laboratory, IPD and OPD departments, 
student will get the opportunity to obs erve and practice patient care 
skills. Through assignments and demonstrations, students will 
acquire skills and see how those skills are applied in diverse settings.  

===== PAGE 339 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 338 
 
 
 
 
Activity 3: Administering Oxygen  
Under the supervision of tutors and/or clinical instructors, 
assign students to do the following: 
• Prepare equipment for the assigned procedure 
• The tutor should prepare a case scenario for the 
relevant procedure 
• Using the manikin/model to administer Oxygen  
• The tutor should asses and provide relevant feedback 
to the student 
 
Activity 1: In the skills laboratory. 
In the above mentioned areas, Divide students in a small 
manageable group and assign to the activities of placing patients in 
different medical positions and administering medications. 
 Using the manikin/model, each student perform given activities. 
 The tutor/instructor should asses and provide relevant feedback 
to the student 
Activity 2: Prepare a tray for common medical and surgical 
procedures 
 Under the supervision of tutors and/or clinical instructors, assign 
students in a small manageable group to prepare trays for 
common medical and surgical procedures.  
 The tutor should asses and provide relevant feedback to the 
student 

===== PAGE 340 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 339 
 
Activity 4: In the IPD and OPD departments 
In the above mentioned areas, Assigned students to the activities of 
administering per rectal diazepam to unconscious patients, administer 
intravenous fluids and position unconscious patients in recovery position. 
The tutor should asses and provide relevant feedback to the student 
 
Activity 5: Performing CPR  
Under the supervision of tutors and/or clinical instructors,  assign 
students to do the following: 
 Prepare equipment for the assigned CPR procedure 
 The tutor should prepare a case scenario for the relevant 
procedure 
 Using the manikin/model, each student perform the CPR 
The tutor should asses and provide relevant feedback to the 
student 
 
Activity 6: Providing First Aid  
Under the supervision of tutors and/or clinical instructors,  assign 
students to do the following: 
 Prepare First Aid kit for the assigned procedure 
 The tutor should prepare a case scenario for the relevant First 
Aid procedures  
 Using the manikin/model, each student perform the First Aid 
procedure assigned by the tutor/clinical instructor  
The tutor should asses and provide relevant feedback to the 
student 
 
  

===== PAGE 341 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 340 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations of students, 
provide support and document progress using checklists provided. 
The clinical instructors and tutors will  give timely feedback for 
student’s improvement. The reports shall be assessed and graded; 
then immediate feedback shall be given to the student.

.

49.5 Position the 
patient in 
Prone 
position 
49.5.1 Procedure 
 Places patient on the abdomen and turn the 
head to one side 
 Makes sure the spine is straight 
 Uses small pillow or a folded bath towel for 
comfort under his head 
 Places the arms flat at patient‘s side or flexed at 
the elbow with the hands near the patient’s 
head 
 Places hand rolls if needed. 
 For tall patients, the feet should extend beyond 
the end of the mattress so that they point down 
in the space between the mattress and foot 
board. With short patients, place a roll under the 
ankles to keep the feet in proper alignment

51.2 Performin
g CPR 
51.2.2Lays the casualty/patient on his back on hard 
surface with head turned to one side 
    
51.2.3Clears the air way by removing any obstructions 
such as vomitus and other debris using gauze or by 
suction 
    
51.2.4Loosens tight clothes around the neck, chest and 
waste 
    
51.2.5Removes dentures (if any)     
51.2.6Kneels/stands beside the patient’s head and 
ensures the patient is lying flat 
    
51.2.7Places one hand under the casualty’s/patient’s neck 
and the other hand under the lower jaw, the head 
and neck are extended backward 
    
51.2.8Using an Ambu bag forcibly inflates air into 
patient’s/casualty’s lungs twice in quick successions 
    
51.2.9Observes whether or not the patient’s chest moves     

===== PAGE 358 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 357 
Skills 
Observed 
Not Observed 
Not Applicable 
Comments 
Standard Criteria 
51.2.10 Checks carotid pulse every two to three 
minutes 
    
51.2.11 If the casualty/patient has no pulse, starts 
external cardiac massage and artificial respiration at 
the ratio of 2 breaths to every 15 chest 
compressions or 1 breath to every 5 compressions 
    
51.2.12 Kneels/stands as close as possible to the 
patient’s side, at right angle to him or her alongside  
the thorax 
    
51.2.13 Places the heels of one hand on patient’s 
sternum and the other hand is placed on top of the 
first, fingers interlaced 
    
51.2.14  Leans directly over the patient with elbows 
extended 
    
51.2.15 Presses down the lower third of the 
sternum sharply; compressing the chest to at least 
    

===== PAGE 359 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 358 
Skills 
Observed 
Not Observed 
Not Applicable 
Comments 
Standard Criteria 
2 inches and releases the chest to recoil 
completely. 
51.2.16 Repeats compressions 30 times at a rate of 
100 compressions per minute while keeping hands 
in place 
    
51.2.17 Inflates air into patient’s chest twice every 
after 30 compressions until pulses return. 
    
51.2.18 Places the patient in recovery position 
when he/she starts breathing spontaneously.  
    
51.2.19 Checks the vital signs every after 5 minutes 
for half an hour, after 15 minutes for 1 hour, after 
30 minutes for 2 hours 
    
51.2.20 Discards any wastes (if any) in appropriate 
containers  
    
51.2.21 Decontaminates reusable equipment and 
returns to their appropriate places 
    
51.2.22 Removes gloves

===== PAGE 365 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 364 
52.2.9 Checks the vial/ampoule label against the medicine card 
(second time) 
    
52.2.10 Removes the metal cap from the rubber seal of the 
vial 
    
52.2.11 If the medicine is in a powder form dilutes it 
according to manufacturer’s instructions 
    
52.2.12 Calculates medication dosage accurately without 
contamination 
    
52.2.13 Checks the vial/ampoule label against the 
medicine card (third time) 
    
52.2.14 Ensures patient/client privacy     
52.2.15 Washes hands with soap and water and puts on 
gloves 
    
52.2.16 Takes a sterile syringe with needle and removes 
the cap and throws cap in the waste bin  
    
52.2.17 Draws the correct amount of medication from the 
vial/ampoule into syringe making sure no air is in syringe 
    
52.2.18 Calls the patient/client by name for verification and 
compares with the treatment card/chart or prescription 
    
52.2.19 Informs the patient/client about the procedure     
52.2.20 Assists the patient/client to the appropriate position 
depending on the chosen site 
    

===== PAGE 366 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 365 
52.2.21 Obtains assistance in holding a restless 
patient/client to prevent injury due to sudden movement after 
needle insertion 
    
52.2.22 Looks for a vein that can be approached. Palpates 
the vein and positions the tourniquet. (if has no IV line insitu) 
    
52.2.23 Asks patient to close and open his hand several 
times to facilitate venous drainage and disinfects the area. 
    
52.2.24 Repositions the needle so that it is with the hollow 
tip pointing upwards and fixes the vein with the non-dominant 
hand. 
    
52.2.25 Punctures the vein at an angle of approximately 
10-30º 
    
52.2.26 Holds barrel of syringe steady with a non-dominant 
hand and aspirates by pulling back on the plunger with a 
dominant hand. If the blood enters the syringe easily, we have 
the confirmation that the needle is inside the vessel  
    
52.2.27 Unfolds the tourniquet. Injects the substance, 
respecting the injection rate specific to each drug. 
    
52.2.28 Pulls the needle out of the vein smoothly the same 
angle of insertion 
    
52.2.29 Compresses the puncture region with a 
disinfectant pad until blood stops flowing. 
    
52.2.30 Asks the patient/client how they feel and thanks 
them

52.3.18 Calls the patient/client by name for verification and 
compares with the treatment card/chart or prescription 
    
52.3.19 Informs the patient/client about the procedure     
52.3.20 Assists the patient/client to the appropriate position

52.3.21 Obtains assistance in holding a restless 
patient/client to prevent injury due to sudden movement after 
needle insertion 
    
52.3.22 Selects, locates and cleans the site     
52.3.23 With the needle at a 90 degree angle while 
pressing down and holding the skin taut, quickly inserts 
needle into tissue 
    
52.3.24 In case of an emaciated patient/client or infant, 
pinches the muscle to make it firmer and facilitate needle 
insertion 
    
52.3.25 Holds barrel of syringe steady with a non-dominant 
hand and aspirates by pulling back on the plunger with a 
dominant hand. If blood appears in the syringe, withdraws 
needle and prepares a new injection  
    
52.3.26 If blood does not appear in the syringe, injects the 
medication steadily and slowly while holding the syringe 
steady 
    
52.3.27 Withdraws needle smoothly the same angle of 
insertion 
    

===== PAGE 370 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 369 
52.3.28 Applies gentle pressure at site with dry swab     
52.3.29 If bleeding occurs, applies pressure with dry sterile 
swab until bleeding stops 
    
52.3.30 Asks the patient/client how they feel and thanks 
them 
    
52.3.31 Discards the needle and syringe in the safety box     
52.3.32 Clears, decontaminates and cleans reusable 
equipment and returns them to proper place 
    
52.3.33 Removes gloves and disposes appropriately     
52.3.34 Washes hands with soap and water .    
52.3.35 Documents relevant information including the time 
of administration, drug name, dose route and patient/client 
name 
    
52.4 Administeri
ng 
subcutaneo
us injection 
52.4.1 Greets the patient/client warmly and creates rapport     
52.4.2 Informs patient/client about the procedure      
52.4.3 Washes hands with soap and water and puts on gloves     
52.4.4 Prepares injection tray with: 
 Prescribed medicines 
 Syringes and needles 
 Galipot 
 Patient/client case note 
 Ampoule file 
 Safety box 
 Treatment chart 
    

===== PAGE 371 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 370 
 Kidney dish 
 Gloves 
 Alcohol (Methylated spirit) 
52.4.5 Correctly reads the medication order      
52.4.6 Checks the vial/ampoule label against the medicine (first 
time) 
    
52.4.7 Checks the expiry date of the medication     
52.4.8 Asks the patient/client about any allergies to the 
prescribed medicine 
    
52.4.9 Washes hands with soap and water      
52.4.10 Checks the vial/ampoule label against the medicine card 
(second time) 
    
52.4.11 Takes a sterile syringe with needle and removes the cap 
from the needle and throws the cap in the waste bin 
    
52.4.12 Removes metal cap from the rubber seal if a vial and if an 
ampoule cuts top with a file and if the ampoule has a 
mark breaks it from the mark 
    
52.4.13 If the medicine is in powder form dilutes it with a sterile 
syringe and solution according to manufacturer 
instructions 
    
52.4.14 Ensures for patient/client privacy     
52.4.15 Calculates medication dosage      
52.4.16 Washes hands and puts on gloves     

===== PAGE 372 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 371 
52.4.17 Draws correct amount of medication from vial into 
the syringe making sure no air is in the syringe 
    
52.4.18 Checks the vial/ampoule label against the 
medicine card (third time) 
    
52.4.19 Calls the patient/client name for verification and 
compares with the treatment card/chart or prescription 
    
52.4.20 Assists the patient/client to the appropriate position 
depending on site 
    
52.4.21 Obtains assistance in holding a restless 
patient/client to prevent injury due to sudden movement 
after needle insertion 
    
52.4.22 Selects, locates and cleans the site     
52.4.23 With the needle at a 45 degree angle while 
pressing down and holds the skin taut slowly inserts the 
needle into the subcutaneous tissue 
    
52.4.24 In case of an emaciated patient client or infant, 
pinches the muscle to make the muscle firmer and 
facilitate needle insertion 
    
52.4.25 Withdraws the needle slowly at same angle as insertion     
52.4.26 Applies gently pressure at the site with a dry swab     
52.4.27 Asks the patient/client how she/he feels and thanks them     
52.4.28 If bleeding occurs she/he applies pressure with dry sterile 
swab until bleeding stops 
    
52.4.29 Discards the needle and syringe in the safety box     

===== PAGE 373 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 372 
52.4.30 Discards the swabs into an appropriate container     
52.4.31 Clears, decontaminates and cleans reusable equipment 
and returns them to their original places 
    
52.4.32 Removes gloves and dispose according to hospital policy     
52.4.33 Washes hands with soap and water

time of administration, drug name, dose, route and 
patient/client’s name 
    
52.4.35 Clears, decontaminates and cleans reusable

52.5 Administeri
ng topical 
medication
s 
52.5.1 Creates good rapport with the patient/client     
52.5.2 Informs the patient/client about the procedure and makes 
them comfortable 
    
52.5.3 Washes hands with soap and water     
52.5.4 Prepares medication trolley including: 
 Prescribed medicines  
 A pair of clean gloves 
 Kidney dish for wastes 
 Medication charts 
    
52.5.5 Reads the prescribed medicines from medication chart 
and selects correct medicines for the patient/client 
    
52.5.6 Compares the label of the medicine container or unit dose 
package against the patient/client prescription. 
    

===== PAGE 374 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 373 
52.5.7 Asks the patient/client about any allergy with the 
prescribed medicine 
    
52.5.8 Checks the expiry date of the medication     
52.5.9 Calls the patient/client’s name and compares with the 
name on the treatment card/chart or prescription 
    
52.5.10 Wash and dry hands thoroughly     
52.5.11 Apply gloves.     
52.5.12 Always apply creams or ointments to clean skin. 
Wash, rinse and dry the affected area 
    
52.5.13 If skin is very dry and flaking, apply topical 
medication while skin is still damp. 
    
52.5.14 If using a tub scoop a small amount using a gloved 
hand. If using a tube squeeze out a small amount into the 
hand. 
    
52.5.15 Let service user know that initial application may 
feel cold. 
    
52.5.16 Apply a small amount of cream gently in the 
direction of hair growth using a gloved hand — do not rub 
vigorously. 
    
52.5.17 When applying a medicated cream (such as a 
steroid cream or ointment) apply sparingly and only to 
affected areas. 
    
52.5.18 Emollients can usually be used more plentifully. 
Apply as directed on the packet, tube or tub. 
    

===== PAGE 375 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical Medicine 374 
52.5.19 Do not try to return unused cream to the tub as this 
may contaminate the cream. 
    
52.5.20 Remove and dispose of gloves and perform hand 
hygiene. 
    
52.5.21 Documents all relevant information including the 
time of administration, drug name, dose, route and 
patient/client’s name. 
    
52.5.22 Clears, decontaminates and cleans reusable 
equipment and returns in their original places

Appendix: Session Overview of Practicum Guides for 
NTA Level 4 
 
Total Session Time: 120 minutes 
Learning Outcomes 
By the end of this session, learners are expected to be able 
to: 
 Define terms: Practicum, Rotation, Placement, 
Community fieldwork, Field Visit 
 Explain the purpose of the Practicum Guides 
 Describe the roles of the members of the practicum 
training team 
 Identify the different practicum resources 
 Describe the organization and contents of the Practicum 
Guides 
 Describe how to use the Practicum Guide  
Resources Needed 
 Flip charts, marker pens, and masking tape 
 Black/white board and chalk/whiteboard markers 
 Practicum Guide for NTA level 4 
 Practical Procedure Books 
 Clinical Rotation plan 
 Computer and LCD projector

EXPLAIN to participants that this session will take 120 
minutes 
 
Step 2: Introduction, Definition of Terms and Purpose 
of Practicum Guides (15 minutes) 
Introduction 
There are three Practicum Guides handbooks for each 
NTA level: 
 A Practicum Guide for NTA Level 4 
 A Practicum Guide for NTA Level 5 
 A Practicum Guide for NTA Level 6 
Practicum guides contain practicum for all modules with 
practical hours.  
 
  

===== PAGE 396 =====

 Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
395 
 
 
Definition of terms: 
Activity: Brainstorming (10 minutes) 
ASK students, “What is a practicum, Rotation, Placement, 
Community fieldwork, Field Visit? 
ALLOW for a few responses. 
WRITE on chalk board or flip charts 
DISTRIBUTE copies of practicum guides to all the students 
and ask them to open the introduction pages where they can 
find the definition of the above terms. 
SUMMARISE using the contents below 
 
Practicum  
 Refers to the part of a course consisting of practical 
work in a particular field. 
 The new curriculum for NTA level 4, 5 and 6 stresses 
on competency based training; it was found necessary 
to standardize both the classroom and the clinical 
training. 
 This overview attempts to create some guidance on 
how training should be standardized in the clinic and 
other settings where students will be attached. 

===== PAGE 397 =====

 Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
396 
 
 
 Rotation/ Placement 
 A period in which students in the clinical part of their 
education passes through various clinical areas for 
short periods of time so that they have opportunity to 
learn the different aspects of care. 
 
 
Field Visit 
 A field visit is an off-school educational experience to 
students which normally involves travelling to a place 
where the students will acquire intended knowledge, 
experience and skills.  
 The field visit is intended to give an opportunity to 
students to experience the actual situation. The field 
visit usually lasts from a few hours to a day or days. 
 
Community Field Work 
 A community fieldwork is an off-school educational 
experience to students which normally involves 
travelling to a selected community setting. 

===== PAGE 398 =====

 Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
397 
 
 
 Community field work aims to provide students with 
opportunities to work at a community level for health 
promotion, so that they can gain experience with 
assessing community needs, planning with the 
community, implementing the plan, and evaluating 
progress with the community.  
Purpose of Practicum Guide 
 Give guidance to tutors, clinical instructors and 
students on clinical teaching and learning 
 Provide standardised clinical/practical learning 
activities across CO schools 
 Improve the quality of teaching and learning in 
clinical/practical settings 
 Monitor students learning in clinical/practical areas 
effectively 
 
  

===== PAGE 399 =====

 Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 4 Clinical 
Medicine 
398

Step 7: How to Use the Practicum Guide (5 minutes) 
 Each learner will get a copy of the practicum guide. 
 Each learner has to understand all the activities and 
assignments intended in each clinical rotation for 
the purpose of learning and practicing skills.  
 Pay close attention to: clinical objectives, 
assignments, monitoring and evaluation tools 
(checklists)  
 Make sure that each learner refers to a relevant 
practicum while in clinical rotation on daily basis. 
 Look at the specific area of practice and review 
skills needed 
 Review the clinical/practical outcomes, 
competencies and the assignments/activities 
 Go to clinical/practical area with some specific 
activity or assignment you want to learn 
 
Step 8: Key Points (5 minutes)

 The Practicum Guide contains clinical/practical 
teaching materials for learning in clinical/practical 
areas  
 All apprenticeship modules and Hospitals attachments 
are included in this practicum guide 
 Tutors, clinical instructors, hospital staff (non-teaching 
staff), students and patients each have an important 
role in the practicum 
 It is very important for tutors, clinical instructors and 
students to read and utilize the Practicum Guide 
 
Step 9: Evaluation (10 minutes) 
 What is a practicum? 
 Who are the Clinical/Practical teaching team? 
 What are their roles? 
 What are the major components of a module 
practicum?
